I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 68 / not_in_workforce / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I do still get up and do ordinary things. But it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s this quiet heaviness in me, and I can’t really reason myself out of it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose so… though I still try to keep up with things. With my watercolors, for example, I still set them out sometimes, and I mean to paint. But then I’ll just sit there looking at the paper, and the feeling I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t really come. So I end up putting it all away. I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say anxious most of the time, no. I mean, I have my moments, like anyone does, and I can get a bit unsettled if I’m not sure I’m doing something right… but it hasn’t felt like I’m on edge all day or anything. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really. I’ve had sad stretches in life, of course, but I don’t know that I’d say they were quite like this. I may just not be remembering it clearly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this same kind of gray film or heaviness before in your life, before these past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in quite this way. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before these past eight months? I mean, I’ve had low times before, certainly, but lately it’s more like most days there’s this gray film over everything. Even ordinary things, like making tea or looking out at the garden, feel sort of muted. There’s a heaviness there, quietly, and I can’t seem to talk myself out of it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly point to, no. I’ve had unhappy periods, like I said, but more than one time like this… I don’t think so. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before these past eight months? I don’t think I’ve had anything that lasted like this for two weeks or more, no. I’ve had sad spells, of course, but this particular thing—most days feeling like a gray film is over everything, even simple things like making tea or looking at the garden feeling heavy—I don’t remember having that before. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt as depressed as you do now before the past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so—not as depressed as this, before these past eight months. I’ve had ordinary sad times, but not this sort of… grayness over most days, where even making tea or looking at the garden has that quiet heaviness to it. I hope I’m answering what you’re asking.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I’ve been feeling a bit concerned about some thoughts I’ve been having. Not constantly, exactly, but that’s when it started—around when my brother’s health and behavior changed so suddenly. I still try to do things, like I’ll set out my watercolor supplies sometimes. But then I just sit there looking at the blank paper, and after a while I put everything away. The enjoyment I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t seem to come, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, not anything I would call significant. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I’ve been feeling a bit concerned about some thoughts I’ve been having. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, and I’m not planning to hurt myself or anything like that, but… what scared me was catching myself thinking, “Maybe it would just be simpler if I didn’t wake up tomorrow.” That didn’t feel like me, and I thought I should probably talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts?\n\nPatient: I think… it’s been about eight months now. Not constantly, exactly, but that’s when it started—around when my brother’s health and behavior changed so suddenly. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that usually bring you pleasure over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. I still try to do things, like I’ll set out my watercolor supplies sometimes. But then I just sit there looking at the blank paper, and after a while I put everything away. The enjoyment I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t seem to come, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, not anything I would call significant. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I haven’t been keeping careful track on a scale, though, so I hope that’s alright to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything very clear there. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently sad or depressed most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I do still get up and do ordinary things. But it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s this quiet heaviness in me, and I can’t really reason myself out of it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose so… though I still try to keep up with things. With my watercolors, for example, I still set them out sometimes, and I mean to paint. But then I’ll just sit there looking at the paper, and the feeling I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t really come. So I end up putting it all away. I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say anxious most of the time, no. I mean, I have my moments, like anyone does, and I can get a bit unsettled if I’m not sure I’m doing something right… but it hasn’t felt like I’m on edge all day or anything. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how much you enjoy activities or things that usually bring you pleasure over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have, though I don’t want to overstate it. I still try to do things, like I’ll set out my watercolor supplies sometimes. But then I just sit there looking at the blank paper, and after a while I put everything away. The enjoyment I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t seem to come, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, not anything I would call significant. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I haven’t been keeping careful track on a scale, though, so I hope that’s alright to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything very clear there. I do get tired, but… I’m older, so I don’t always know what to count as unusual. I don’t think I’m moving especially slowly or anything like that—at least no one’s mentioned it to me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently sad or depressed most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I do still get up and do ordinary things. But it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s this quiet heaviness in me, and I can’t really reason myself out of it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose so… though I still try to keep up with things. With my watercolors, for example, I still set them out sometimes, and I mean to paint. But then I’ll just sit there looking at the paper, and the feeling I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t really come. So I end up putting it all away. I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say anxious most of the time, no. I mean, I have my moments, like anyone does, and I can get a bit unsettled if I’m not sure I’m doing something right… but it hasn’t felt like I’m on edge all day or anything. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really. I’ve had sad stretches in life, of course, but I don’t know that I’d say they were quite like this. I may just not be remembering it clearly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this same kind of gray film or heaviness before in your life, before these past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in quite this way. I mean, I’ve had low times before, certainly, but lately it’s more like most days there’s this gray film over everything. Even ordinary things, like making tea or looking out at the garden, feel sort of muted. There’s a heaviness there, quietly, and I can’t seem to talk myself out of it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly point to, no. I’ve had unhappy periods, like I said, but more than one time like this… I don’t think so. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before these past eight months? I don’t think I’ve had anything that lasted like this for two weeks or more, no. I’ve had sad spells, of course, but this particular thing—most days feeling like a gray film is over everything, even simple things like making tea or looking at the garden feeling heavy—I don’t remember having that before. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt as depressed as you do now before the past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so—not as depressed as this, before these past eight months. I’ve had ordinary sad times, but not this sort of… grayness over most days, where even making tea or looking at the garden has that quiet heaviness to it. I hope I’m answering what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that were similar to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I had anything similar before these past eight months. I’ve had low moments, of course, but not this feeling where most days it’s like a gray film has settled over everything. Even little things, like making tea or looking at the garden, have this quiet heaviness to them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depressive feelings have been interfering with your daily life a lot, or just a little?\n\nPatient: I suppose… more than just a little, though I’m still managing the basics. I keep the house going and take care of the essentials. But I have pulled back from things I used to do, like my walking group and the mentoring. I just can’t seem to follow through, or when I’m with people I don’t feel fully present the way I used to. It feels a bit embarrassing to say, because I’m not completely unable to function, but it has affected things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depressive feelings as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say mild… I mean, it’s there most days, like that gray film over everything, and even making tea or looking out at my garden can have this quiet heaviness to it. But I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Mild seems right, I think—if that’s the kind of scale you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you're currently doing, even if it's not as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I do still try. I’ll set out my watercolor things sometimes, and I think, “Well, this will be nice.” But then I just sit there looking at the paper, and the pleasure I used to get from the colors and the light doesn’t really come. After a while I usually just put it all away. So… there’s some trying, but not much enjoyment, if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty sleeping or been sleeping more than usual over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think sleep has been a bit unsettled. Not terribly, I don’t want to overstate it, but I don’t sleep as soundly as I used to. I’ll fall asleep some nights, then wake up around three or four and start thinking about phone calls I need to make, forms, whether I handled something the right way… little things like that. And then by morning I feel as if I’ve hardly rested, even if I was in bed long enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates": ["F32.0", "F32.1"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

I haven’t been keeping careful track on a scale, though, so I hope that’s alright to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before these thoughts started?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything very clear there. I do get tired, but… I’m older, so I don’t always know what to count as unusual. I don’t think I’m moving especially slowly or anything like that—at least no one’s mentioned it to me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt consistently sad or depressed most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… most days, I think. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I do still get up and do ordinary things. But it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s this quiet heaviness in me, and I can’t really reason myself out of it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose so… though I still try to keep up with things. With my watercolors, for example, I still set them out sometimes, and I mean to paint. But then I’ll just sit there looking at the paper, and the feeling I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t really come. So I end up putting it all away. I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say anxious most of the time, no. I mean, I have my moments, like anyone does, and I can get a bit unsettled if I’m not sure I’m doing something right… but it hasn’t felt like I’m on edge all day or anything. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really. I’ve had sad stretches in life, of course, but I don’t know that I’d say they were quite like this. I may just not be remembering it clearly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this same kind of gray film or heaviness before in your life, before these past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in quite this way. I mean, I’ve had low times before, certainly, but lately it’s more like most days there’s this gray film over everything. Even ordinary things, like making tea or looking out at the garden, feel sort of muted. There’s a heaviness there, quietly, and I can’t seem to talk myself out of it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly point to, no. I’ve had unhappy periods, like I said, but more than one time like this… I don’t think so. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before these past eight months? I don’t think I’ve had anything that lasted like this for two weeks or more, no. I’ve had sad spells, of course, but this particular thing—most days feeling like a gray film is over everything, even simple things like making tea or looking at the garden feeling heavy—I don’t remember having that before. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt as depressed as you do now before the past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so—not as depressed as this, before these past eight months. I’ve had ordinary sad times, but not this sort of… grayness over most days, where even making tea or looking at the garden has that quiet heaviness to it. I hope I’m answering what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that were similar to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I had anything similar before these past eight months. I’ve had low moments, of course, but not this feeling where most days it’s like a gray film has settled over everything. Even little things, like making tea or looking at the garden, have this quiet heaviness to them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depressive feelings have been interfering with your daily life a lot, or just a little?\n\nPatient: I suppose… more than just a little, though I’m still managing the basics. I keep the house going and take care of the essentials. But I have pulled back from things I used to do, like my walking group and the mentoring. I just can’t seem to follow through, or when I’m with people I don’t feel fully present the way I used to. It feels a bit embarrassing to say, because I’m not completely unable to function, but it has affected things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depressive feelings as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say mild… I mean, it’s there most days, like that gray film over everything, and even making tea or looking out at my garden can have this quiet heaviness to it. But I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Mild seems right, I think—if that’s the kind of scale you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you're currently doing, even if it's not as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I do still try. I’ll set out my watercolor things sometimes, and I think, “Well, this will be nice.” But then I just sit there looking at the paper, and the pleasure I used to get from the colors and the light doesn’t really come. After a while I usually just put it all away. So… there’s some trying, but not much enjoyment, if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty sleeping or been sleeping more than usual over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think sleep has been a bit unsettled. Not terribly, I don’t want to overstate it, but I don’t sleep as soundly as I used to. I’ll fall asleep some nights, then wake up around three or four and start thinking about phone calls I need to make, forms, whether I handled something the right way… little things like that. And then by morning I feel as if I’ve hardly rested, even if I was in bed long enough.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your depressive feelings have been present nearly every day over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think nearly every day is fair. It’s not always dramatic or anything, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over things. Even just making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s a quiet heaviness in me. I try to reason myself out of it, but it doesn’t really lift. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I do still get up and do ordinary things. But it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s this quiet heaviness in me, and I can’t really reason myself out of it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in activities you used to enjoy over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose so… though I still try to keep up with things. With my watercolors, for example, I still set them out sometimes, and I mean to paint. But then I’ll just sit there looking at the paper, and the feeling I used to get from the colors and the light… it just doesn’t really come. So I end up putting it all away. I don’t know if that counts as less interest, but it feels different than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge most of the time over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say anxious most of the time, no. I mean, I have my moments, like anyone does, and I can get a bit unsettled if I’m not sure I’m doing something right… but it hasn’t felt like I’m on edge all day or anything. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling depressed like this before in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, really. I’ve had sad stretches in life, of course, but I don’t know that I’d say they were quite like this. I may just not be remembering it clearly. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this same kind of gray film or heaviness before in your life, before these past eight months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in quite this way. I mean, I’ve had low times before, certainly, but lately it’s more like most days there’s this gray film over everything. Even ordinary things, like making tea or looking out at the garden, feel sort of muted. There’s a heaviness there, quietly, and I can’t seem to talk myself out of it. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt depressed like this before?\n\nPatient: Not that I can clearly point to, no. I’ve had unhappy periods, like I said, but more than one time like this… I don’t think so. I wouldn’t want to say yes if I’m not sure. Does that answer it properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before these past eight months? I don’t think I’ve had anything that lasted like this for two weeks or more, no. I’ve had sad spells, of course, but this particular thing—most days feeling like a gray film is over everything, even simple things like making tea or looking at the garden feeling heavy—I don’t remember having that before. I hope that’s clear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period in your life where you felt as depressed as you do now before the past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so—not as depressed as this, before these past eight months. I’ve had ordinary sad times, but not this sort of… grayness over most days, where even making tea or looking at the garden has that quiet heaviness to it. I hope I’m answering what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of feeling significantly depressed before the past eight months that were similar to how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I had anything similar before these past eight months. I’ve had low moments, of course, but not this feeling where most days it’s like a gray film has settled over everything. Even little things, like making tea or looking at the garden, have this quiet heaviness to them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depressive feelings have been interfering with your daily life a lot, or just a little?\n\nPatient: I suppose… more than just a little, though I’m still managing the basics. I keep the house going and take care of the essentials. But I have pulled back from things I used to do, like my walking group and the mentoring. I just can’t seem to follow through, or when I’m with people I don’t feel fully present the way I used to. It feels a bit embarrassing to say, because I’m not completely unable to function, but it has affected things.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current depressive feelings as mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably say mild… I mean, it’s there most days, like that gray film over everything, and even making tea or looking out at my garden can have this quiet heaviness to it. But I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Mild seems right, I think—if that’s the kind of scale you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you're currently doing, even if it's not as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I do still try. I’ll set out my watercolor things sometimes, and I think, “Well, this will be nice.” But then I just sit there looking at the paper, and the pleasure I used to get from the colors and the light doesn’t really come. After a while I usually just put it all away. So… there’s some trying, but not much enjoyment, if that answers it properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty sleeping or been sleeping more than usual over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think sleep has been a bit unsettled. Not terribly, I don’t want to overstate it, but I don’t sleep as soundly as I used to. I’ll fall asleep some nights, then wake up around three or four and start thinking about phone calls I need to make, forms, whether I handled something the right way… little things like that. And then by morning I feel as if I’ve hardly rested, even if I was in bed long enough.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your depressive feelings have been present nearly every day over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think nearly every day is fair. It’s not always dramatic or anything, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over things. Even just making tea or looking out at the garden, there’s a quiet heaviness in me. I try to reason myself out of it, but it doesn’t really lift. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depressive feelings over the past eight months have been consistently mild or have there been periods where they felt worse?\n\nPatient: I’d say… mostly mild, I suppose, though that almost sounds tidier than it feels. It’s there most days, like I said—this gray film over everything. Even simple things, like making tea or looking out at the garden, have this quiet heaviness to them. I can’t quite reason my way out of it, even though I do try. Some days feel a bit heavier than others, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
